Table  13  
Use  Case  Report  –  Verify  Customer  Requirements  
 
Use  Case  Name  Verify  Customer  Requirements  
Actors  Customer  /  Renter;  Owner  /  Admin  
Description  This  use  case  allows  the  customer/renter  to  submit  required  renter  information  and  documents,  and  allows  the  owner/admin  to  review  and  record  the  verification  status.  
Precondition  A  customer  record  or  reservation  record  exists  and  the  required  renter  information  or  documents  are  ready  for  submission  or  review.  
Flow  of  Events  Customer  /  Renter  Owner  /  Admin  
1.  The  customer/renter  opens  the  requirements  page.  
1.  The  owner/admin  opens  the  requirements  page.  
2.  The  customer/renter  submits  renter  information.  
2.  The  owner/admin  reviews  submitted  renter  information.  
3.  The  customer/renter  uploads  required  documents.  
3.  The  owner/admin  reviews  submitted  requirements.  
4.  The  customer/renter  views  the  requirement  status  when  available.  
4.  The  owner/admin  records  the  verification  status.  
Post  Conditions  The  customer  requirement  verification  status  is  recorded  and  can  be  used  for  reservation  approval  and  payment  verification.  
The  customer  requirement  verification  status  is  recorded  and  can  be  used  for  reservation  approval  and  payment  verification.  
Exceptions  1.  If  required  documents  are  missing  or  unreadable,  the  system  prompts  the  customer/renter  to  provide  corrected  requirements.  2.  If  submitted  information  is  inconsistent,  the  owner/admin  records  the  appropriate  verification  status.   
 
 

===== PAGE 187 =====
180   
Table  14  
Use  Case  Report  –  Verify  Payment  
Use  Case  Name  Verify  Payment  
Actors  Customer  /  Renter;  Owner  /  Admin  
Description  This  use  case  allows  the  customer/renter  to  submit  a  payment  reference  and  allows  the  owner/admin  to  review  payment  proof,  check  the  reference,  and  record  the  payment  status.  
Precondition  A  reservation  or  rental  transaction  exists,  and  the  customer/renter  has  payment  proof  or  reference  information  available.  
Flow  of  Events  Customer  /  Renter  Owner  /  Admin  
1.  The  customer/renter  opens  the  payment  records  page.  
1.  The  owner/admin  opens  the  payment  records  module.  
2.  The  customer/renter  submits  the  payment  reference.  
2.  The  owner/admin  reviews  the  payment  proof.  
3.  The  customer/renter  waits  for  the  payment  status  update.  
3.  The  owner/admin  checks  the  payment  reference.  
4.  The  customer/renter  views  the  payment  status  when  available.  
4.  The  owner/admin  records  the  payment  status.  
Post  Conditions  The  payment  status  is  recorded  and  can  be  used  for  booking  confirmation,  vehicle  assignment,  rental  transaction  processing,  and  settlement.  
Exceptions  1.  If  the  payment  proof  is  unclear  or  incomplete,  the  system  prompts  the  customer/renter  to  submit  corrected  proof.  2.  If  the  payment  reference  cannot  be  confirmed,  the  owner/admin  records  the  payment  status  as  pending  or  invalid.   
 
 

===== PAGE 188 =====
181   
Table  15  
Use  Case  Report  –  Manage  Rental  Transactions  
Use  Case  Name  Manage  Rental  Transactions  
Actors  Customer  /  Renter;  Owner  /  Admin  
Description  This  use  case  manages  the  active  rental  transaction  from  confirmed  reservation  and  vehicle  turnover  through  rental  status  tracking  and  transaction  history  review.  
Precondition  A  reservation  is  confirmed,  a  vehicle  is  assigned,  and  the  required  payment  or  requirement  status  is  valid  for  rental  processing.  
Flow  of  Events  Customer  /  Renter  Owner  /  Admin  
1.  The  customer/renter  participates  in  the  rental  transaction  process.  
1.  The  owner/admin  opens  the  rental  transaction  record.  
2.  The  customer/renter  confirms  the  booking  information  when  requested.  
2.  The  owner/admin  reviews  booking  information.  
3.  The  customer/renter  acknowledges  vehicle  and  payment  details  when  needed.  
3.  The  owner/admin  reviews  vehicle  and  payment  details.  
4.  The  customer/renter  receives  or  returns  the  vehicle  during  turnover.  
4.  The  owner/admin  confirms  vehicle  turnover.  
5.  The  customer/renter  views  the  transaction  status  when  available.  
5.  The  owner/admin  updates  rental  status  and  views  transaction  history.  
Post  Conditions  The  rental  transaction  is  recorded  and  becomes  part  of  the  operational  history  used  for  monitoring,  reporting,  and  demand  forecasting.  
Exceptions  1.  If  required  transaction  details  are  missing,  the  system  prompts  the  owner/admin  to  complete  the  record.  2.  If  requirement  or  payment  status  is  not  valid,  the  rental  transaction  cannot  proceed  until  the  status  is  resolved.   
 
 

===== PAGE 189 =====
182   
Table  16  
Use  Case  Report  –  Process  Vehicle  Return  
Use  Case  Name  Process  Vehicle  Return  
Actors  Owner  /  Admin  
Description  This  use  case  allows  the  owner/admin  to  process  the  return  of  a  rented  vehicle  by  recording  return  details,  reviewing  vehicle  condition,  checking  remaining  balance,  and  recording  settlement  information.  
Precondition  An  active  rental  transaction  exists  and  the  rented  vehicle  is  due  for  return  or  presented  for  return.  
Flow  of  Events  1.  The  owner/admin  opens  the  active  rental  record.  2.  The  owner/admin  records  the  return  date  and  time.  3.  The  owner/admin  reviews  the  vehicle  condition.  4.  The  owner/admin  reviews  the  remaining  balance.  5.  The  owner/admin  records  the  return  settlement.  
Post  Conditions  The  return  details  and  settlement  status  are  recorded  for  rental  closure,  vehicle  availability  review,  and  customer  rental  history.  
Exceptions  1.  If  return  details  are  incomplete,  the  system  prompts  the  owner/admin  to  complete  the  required  fields.  2.  If  damage,  penalty,  or  unsettled  balance  is  identified,  the  owner/admin  records  the  needed  settlement  details  before  finalizing  the  return.   
 
 

===== PAGE 190 =====
183   
Table  17  
Use  Case  Report  –  View  Forecasted  Demand  
Use  Case  Name  View  Forecasted  Demand  
Actors  Owner  /  Admin  
Description  This  use  case  allows  the  owner/admin  to  view  forecasted  rental  demand  by  selecting  branch,  vehicle  category,  and  forecast  period.  
Precondition  The  owner/admin  is  logged  in  and  historical  rental  data  or  sample  forecasting  data  are  available.  
Flow  of  Events  1.  The  owner/admin  opens  the  forecasting  module.  2.  The  owner/admin  selects  the  branch  and  vehicle  category.  3.  The  owner/admin  sets  the  forecast  period.  4.  The  owner/admin  reviews  the  forecasted  demand.  5.  The  owner/admin  reviews  the  branch  demand  trend.  
Post  Conditions  The  owner/admin  views  forecasted  demand  results  that  may  support  reports  and  fleet  allocation  recommendations.  
Exceptions  1.  If  historical  data  are  insufficient,  the  system  informs  the  owner/admin  that  the  forecast  may  be  limited.  2.  If  the  selected  period  or  category  is  invalid,  the  system  prompts  the  owner/admin  to  revise  the  selection.   
 
 

===== PAGE 191 =====
184   
Table  18  
Use  Case  Report  –  View  Fleet  Allocation  Recommendation  
Use  Case  Name  View  Fleet  Allocation  Recommendation  
Actors  Owner  /  Admin  
Description  This  use  case  allows  the  owner/admin  to  review  advisory  fleet  allocation  recommendations  based  on  allocation  criteria,  vehicle  availability,  contextual  factors,  and  operational  needs.  
Precondition  The  owner/admin  is  logged  in  and  vehicle  records,  availability  records,  forecasting  data,  and  allocation  criteria  are  available.  
Flow  of  Events  1.  The  owner/admin  opens  the  allocation  recommendation  module.  2.  The  owner/admin  selects  allocation  criteria.  3.  The  owner/admin  reviews  vehicle  availability.  4.  The  owner/admin  reviews  contextual  factors.  5.  The  owner/admin  reviews  the  recommended  allocation.  6.  The  owner/admin  decides  the  final  allocation  action.  
Post  Conditions  The  owner/admin  reviews  the  recommended  allocation  and  may  use  it  as  decision-support  information  for  fleet  planning  or  vehicle  assignment.  
Exceptions  1.  If  required  forecasting,  availability,  or  contextual  information  is  insufficient,  the  system  displays  only  the  available  recommendation  details.  2.  If  no  suitable  vehicle  is  found,  the  owner/admin  may  revise  the  allocation  criteria.

Table  19  
Use  Case  Report  –  View  Generated  Reports   
Use  Case  Name  View  Generated  Reports  
Actors  Owner  /  Admin  
Description  This  use  case  allows  the  owner/admin  to  select  report  criteria,  set  filters,  view  report  output,  and  review  operational  results  for  monitoring  and  decision-making.  
Precondition  The  owner/admin  is  logged  in  and  relevant  operational  records  are  available  in  the  system.  
Flow  of  Events  1.  The  owner/admin  opens  the  reports  module.  2.  The  owner/admin  selects  the  report  type.  3.  The  owner/admin  sets  the  report  filters.  4.  The  owner/admin  views  the  report  output.  5.  The  owner/admin  reviews  the  report  results.  
Post  Conditions  The  owner/admin  views  the  generated  report  for  operational  monitoring,  planning,  documentation,  or  decision-making.  
Exceptions  1.  If  no  records  match  the  selected  filters,  the  system  informs  the  owner/admin  that  no  report  data  are  available.  2.  If  the  report  criteria  are  invalid,  the  system  prompts  the  owner/admin  to  revise  the  selected  filters.

Table  20  
Use  Case  Report  –  Configure  Notifications  
Use  Case  Name  Configure  Notifications  
Actors  Owner  /  Admin  
Description  This  use  case  allows  the  owner/admin  to  configure  booking  update  alerts,  verification  result  alerts,  payment  reminders,  return  schedule  alerts,  maintenance  reminders,  and  low  availability  alerts.  
Precondition  The  owner/admin  is  logged  in  and  notification  settings  or  alert  rules  are  available  for  configuration.  
Flow  of  Events  1.  The  owner/admin  opens  the  notification  settings.  2.  The  owner/admin  configures  booking  update  alerts.  3.  The  owner/admin  configures  verification  result  alerts.  4.  The  owner/admin  configures  payment  reminders.  5.  The  owner/admin  configures  return  schedule  alerts.  6.  The  owner/admin  configures  maintenance  reminders.  7.  The  owner/admin  configures  low  availability  alerts.  
Post  Conditions  Notification  and  alert  rules  are  configured  and  can  be  used  for  customer  booking  updates  and  operational  reminders.  
Exceptions  1.  If  notification  settings  are  incomplete,  the  system  prompts  the  owner/admin  to  complete  the  required  settings.  2.  If  alert  criteria  are  incorrectly  configured,  the  system  prompts  the  owner/admin  to  revise  the  criteria  before  saving.

in

Table  55  
Data  Dictionary  -  Demand  Forecasts  
Table  Name:  Demand_Forecasts  
Attributes  Data  Type  Field  Size  Description  
forecast_id  INT  11  Primary  key,  unique  identifier  for  each  weekly  demand  forecast  record.  forecast_run_id  VARCHAR  36  Shared  identifier  used  to  group  the  three  weekly  forecast  records  generated  during  the  same  forecasting  run.  branch_id  INT  11  Foreign  key  that  identifies  the  branch  covered  by  the  forecast.

===== PAGE 266 =====
259   
forecast_horizon_week  INT  11  Forecast  horizon  represented  by  the  record:  1  for  Week  +1,  2  for  Week  +2,  or  3  for  Week  +3.  forecast_period_start  DATE    Start  date  of  the  one-week  forecast  period.  forecast_period_end  DATE    End  date  of  the  one-week  forecast  period.  method  ENUM    Forecasting  method  used  by  the  system,  specifically  Weighted  Moving  Average.  
forecasted_demand  DECIMAL  10,2  WMA-estimated  qualifying  vehicle  demand  for  the  branch  and  vehicle  category  during  the  forecast  period.   required_vehicle_units  INT  11  Whole-number  vehicle  requirement  obtained  by  rounding  the  forecasted  demand  upward  for  branch-allocation  analysis.   actual_demand  INT  11  Actual  qualifying  booking  demand  recorded  for  the  same  branch,  vehicle  category,  and  weekly  period  after  completion  of  the  forecast  period.   percentage_error  DECIMAL  5,2  Absolute  percentage  error  of  the  forecast  when  corresponding  actual  demand  is  greater  than  zero.  The  value  remains  null  when  actual  demand  is  
 

===== PAGE 267 =====
260   
unavailable  or  equal  to  zero.   generated_by  INT  11  Foreign  key  that  identifies  the  Owner/Admin  who  generated  or  initiated  the  forecast.  
generated_at  TIMESTAMP    Date  and  time  the  forecast  was  generated.

Actual  observations  are  whole-number  counts,  while  recursively  used  forecasts  may  contain  decimal  values.  assigned_weight  DECIMAL  5,2  WMA  weight  assigned  to  the  historical  observation:  0.50,  0.30,  or  0.20  according  to  recency.   weighted_value  DECIMAL  10,2  Weighted  contribution  computed  by  multiplying  historical  demand  by  its  assigned  weight.

Operational  and  Decision-Support  Logic  
This  section  presents  the  operational  rules,  computational  procedures,  and  
decision-support
 
logic
 
used
 
by
 
the
 
proposed
 
system.
 
These
 
mechanisms
 
define
 
how
 
the
 
system
 
transforms
 
booking,
 
vehicle,
 
maintenance,
 
forecasting,
 
and
 
contextual
 
data
 
into
 
analytical
 
results
 
and
 
recommendations.
 
The
 
proposed
 
system
 
uses
 
deterministic
 
business
 
rules,
 
statistical
 
computation,
 
and
 
rule-based
 
decision
 
support
 
appropriate
 
to
 
 

===== PAGE 281 =====
274   
the  operational  requirements  of  a  small-scale  multi-branch  car  rental  business.  
Rule-based
 
decision-support
 
approaches
 
are
 
suitable
 
for
 
operational
 
environments
 
where
 
decision
 
criteria
 
must
 
remain
 
explicit,
 
interpretable,
 
and
 
auditable
 
rather
 
than
 
dependent
 
on
 
complex
 
or
 
opaque
 
models
 
(Haber
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026;
 
Mavrin
 
&
 
Makarova,
 
2026;
 
Meira
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Mohamad
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026).
 
The
 
generated
 
outputs
 
are
 
intended
 
to
 
support
 
decision-making
 
and
 
do
 
not
 
replace
 
the
 
judgment
 
of
 
the
 
Owner/Admin.
 
Final
 
decisions
 
regarding
 
booking
 
approval,
 
payment
 
verification,
 
vehicle
 
assignment,
 
vehicle
 
transfer,
 
branch
 
allocation,
 
and
 
other
 
critical
 
operational
 
actions
 
remain
 
under
 
authorized
 
personnel.
 
A.  Demand  Definition  and  Weekly  Data  Aggregation  
Historical  rental  and  booking  information  can  be  used  to  identify  demand  
patterns
 
and
 
support
 
short-term
 
planning
 
and
 
vehicle-allocation
 
decisions
 
in
 
transportation
 
and
 
rental-related
 
operations
 
(Suganya
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026;
 
Zhao
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021;
 
Martin
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021;
 
Huang
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021).
 
In
 
this
 
study,
 
vehicle
 
demand
 
shall
 
be
 
represented
 
by
 
the
 
number
 
of
 
qualifying
 
rental
 
bookings
 
recorded
 
for
 
a
 
specific
 
branch
 
and
 
vehicle
 
category
 
during
 
a
 
calendar
 
week.
 
A  booking  shall  contribute  to  historical  demand  when  it  has  been  accepted  or  
confirmed
 
as
 
a
 
valid
 
rental
 
request
 
for
 
the
 
corresponding
 
branch,
 
vehicle
 
category,
 
and
 
rental
 
schedule.
 
Rejected
 
requests,
 
duplicate
 
requests,
 
and
 
customer-cancelled
 
requests
 
that
 
did
 
not
 
proceed
 
as
 
valid
 
rental
 
transactions
 
shall
 
not
 
be
 
included
 
in
 
the
 
demand
 
count.
 
Weekly  aggregation  is  adopted  because  the  forecasting  component  is  intended  
to
 
support
 
short-term
 
operational
 
planning
 
rather
 
than
 
long-term
 
strategic
 
forecasting.

Each  weekly  demand  value  shall  represent  the  number  of  qualifying  bookings  whose  
scheduled
 
rental
 
start
 
date
 
falls
 
within
 
the
 
corresponding
 
calendar
 
week.

 𝐷

𝑐

𝑡

B.  Weighted  Moving  Average  Demand  Forecasting  
The  proposed  system  shall  use  the  Weighted  Moving  Average  (WMA)  method  
to
 
generate
 
short-term
 
weekly
 
demand
 
forecasts
 
for
 
each
 
branch
 
and
 
vehicle
 
category.
 
WMA
 
assigns
 
different
 
weights
 
to
 
recent
 
observations
 
and
 
gives
 
greater
 
influence
 
to
 
more
 
recent
 
demand
 
information.
 
The
 
reviewed
 
literature
 
supports
 
WMA
 
as
 
a
 
practical,
 
interpretable,
 
and
 
computationally
 
lightweight
 
method
 
for
 
short-term
 
forecasting,
 
particularly
 
where
 
historical
 
data
 
and
 
computational
 
resources
 
are
 
limited
 
(Rahayu
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Sariati
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026;
 
Paramitha
 
&
 
Saifuddin,
 
2023;
 
Khalid,
 
2024).
 
Based  on  this  principle,  the  study  adopts  a  three-period  WMA  using  fixed  
descending
 
weights
 
of
 
0.50,
 
0.30,
 
and
 
0.20.
 
The
 
system
 
shall
 
maintain
 
a
 
rolling
 
forecast
 
horizon
 
covering
 
up
 
to
 
three
 
succeeding
 
weekly
 
periods
 
for
 
each
 
branch
 
and
 
vehicle
 
category.
 
The  first  forecasted  week  shall  be  calculated  entirely  from  the  three  most  
recently
 
completed
 
actual
 
weekly
 
demand
 
observations:

Each  forecast  value  may  contain  a  decimal  because  it  represents  an  estimated  
demand
 
level.
 
Decimal
 
values
 
shall
 
be
 
retained
 
for
 
analytical
 
reporting,
 
historical
 
comparison,
 
and
 
forecast
 
visualization.
 
When

is
 
used
 
to
 
determine
 
the

for

shall
 
be
 
rounded
 
upward
 
to
 
the
 
nearest
 
whole

 𝑅𝑒𝑞𝑢𝑖𝑟𝑒𝑑𝑉𝑒ℎ𝑖𝑐𝑙𝑒𝑠𝑡 + ℎ  = ⌈  𝐹𝑡 + ℎ ⌉ ,  ℎ  ϵ  { 1 ,  2 ,  3 }
The  system  shall  regenerate  the  three-week  forecast  horizon  as  new  completed  
weekly
 
demand
 
becomes
 
available.
 
Consequently,
 
the

may
 
display

together

Forecasted

The

on

progressively
 
incorporate
 
forecasted

and
 
shall
 
therefore
 
be
 
treated
 
as
 
longer-horizon

projections
 
rather
 
than
 
guaranteed
 
demand.
 
All  WMA  outputs  shall  remain  advisory  and  shall  not  automatically  trigger  
vehicle
 
transfer,
 
branch
 
reassignment,
 
or
 
any
 
other
 
operational
 
action.
 
C.  Forecast  Evaluation  Using  Mean  Absolute  Percentage  Error  
Mean  Absolute  Percentage  Error  (MAPE)  shall  be  used  to  evaluate  the  difference  
between
 
forecasted
 
demand
 
and
 
the
 
corresponding
 
actual
 
demand
 
after
 
the
 
forecast
 
period.
 
MAPE
 
expresses
 
the
 
average
 
absolute
 
forecasting
 
error
 
as
 
a
 
percentage
 
of
 
actual
 
observed
 
demand
 
and
 
is
 
commonly
 
used
 
in
 
the
 
evaluation
 
of
 
forecasting
 
models
 
(Rahayu
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022).
 
MAPE  shall  be  computed  as:

 =  actual  demand  during  the  evaluated  period;  𝐴

 =  forecasted  demand  for  the  corresponding  period;  and   𝐹
𝑡
 =  number  of  valid  forecast  observations  included  in  the  calculation.  𝑛
 MAPE  shall  only  be  calculated  for  observations  where  actual  demand  is  greater

zero

percentage

are
 
derived

verified
 
historical
 
client

Demonstration
 
or

shall
 
not

as

of

rather

one-week,

and

three-week  projection  errors  into  a  single  measure.  The  second-  and  third-week

may
 
be

for

and

but
 
shall
 
not
 
be
 
included
 
in
 
the
 
primary
 
MAPE
 
unless
 
forecast
 
performance
 
by
 
horizon
 
is
 
evaluated
 
separately.
 
D.  Vehicle  Utilization  Analysis  
Vehicle  utilization  monitoring  supports  fleet  planning  by  helping  administrators  
identify
 
frequently
 
used,
 
underutilized,
 
and
 
idle
 
fleet
 
units
 
and
 
by
 
providing
 
information
 
useful
 
for
 
fleet
 
deployment
 
decisions.
 
Studies
 
in
 
shared
 
mobility
 
and
 
fleet
 
management
 
likewise
 
show
 
that
 
historical
 
vehicle
 
usage
 
and
 
utilization
 
indicators
 
can
 
support
 
resource
 
planning
 
and
 
operational
 
decision-making
 
(Abouelela
 
et
 
al.,
 
2023;
 
Eliyan
 
&
 
Kerbache,
 
2024).
 
In  the  proposed  system,  vehicle  utilization  shall  measure  the  proportion  of  
eligible
 
operational
 
days
 
within
 
a
 
selected
 
reporting
 
period
 
during
 
which
 
a
 
vehicle
 
was
 
actually
 
used
 
for
 
rental
 
operations.
 
Vehicle  utilization  is  computed  as:  
 
𝑉𝑒ℎ𝑖𝑐𝑙𝑒𝑈𝑡𝑖𝑙𝑖𝑧𝑎𝑡𝑖𝑜𝑛
(
%
)
 
=
 
 𝑅𝑒𝑛𝑡𝑎𝑙𝐷𝑎𝑦𝑠
𝐸𝑙𝑖𝑔𝑖𝑏𝑙𝑒𝑂𝑝𝑒𝑟𝑎𝑡𝑖𝑜𝑛𝑎𝑙𝐷𝑎𝑦𝑠

Rental  Days  refer  to  the  number  of  calendar  days  within  the  reporting  period  
during

the

was

with

Eligible
 
Operational

refer
 
to

during

for
 
rental

Days  when  the  vehicle  was  inactive  or  unavailable  because  of  maintenance

not
 
have

been  offered  for  rental  during  those  periods.  A  reservation  that  does  not  proceed  into

The  administrative  dashboard  may  use  the  most  recent  30-day  period  as  the

or

E.  Idle  Vehicle  Detection  
Idle-vehicle  monitoring  is  relevant  to  fleet  management  because  prolonged  
underutilization
 
may
 
indicate
 
that
 
a
 
vehicle
 
is
 
positioned
 
in
 
a
 
location
 
where
 
available
 
supply
 
exceeds
 
current
 
operational
 
use.
 
Research
 
on
 
fleet
 
relocation
 
and
 
vehicle
 
rebalancing
 
shows
 
that
 
demand,
 
availability,
 
and
 
idle-vehicle
 
information
 
can
 
support
 
improved
 
fleet-distribution
 
decisions
 
(Eliyan
 
&
 
Kerbache,
 
2024;
 
Zhu
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Skrzynski
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026).
 
In  this  study,  idle  vehicle  detection  shall  identify  vehicles  that  remain  
operationally
 
available
 
but
 
have
 
recorded
 
no
 
rental
 
activity
 
for
 
a
 
defined
 
period.
 
A  vehicle  shall  only  be  eligible  for  idle  classification  when  it  is  active,  
rental-ready,
 
not
 
currently
 
rented,
 
not
 
unavailable
 
because
 
of
 
maintenance,
 
and
 
not
 
otherwise
 
prevented
 
from
 
being
 
offered
 
for
 
rental.
 
Idle  duration  shall  be  computed  as:  
 𝐼𝑑𝑙𝑒𝐷𝑎𝑦𝑠  =  𝐶𝑢𝑟𝑟𝑒𝑛𝑡𝐷𝑎𝑡𝑒  −  𝐿𝑎𝑠𝑡𝑅𝑒𝑛𝑡𝑎𝑙𝐸𝑛𝑑𝐷𝑎𝑡𝑒
 

===== PAGE 288 =====
281   
For  a  vehicle  with  no  previous  rental  record,  the  date  when  the  vehicle  became  
operationally
 
available
 
may
 
be
 
used
 
as
 
the
 
reference
 
date.
 
For  purposes  of  the  proposed  system,  an  eligible  vehicle  shall  be  classified  as  
idle
 
when
 
it
 
has
 
recorded
 
no
 
rental
 
activity
 
for
 
at
 
least
 
fourteen
 
consecutive
 
days:
 
 𝐼𝑑𝑙𝑒𝑉𝑒ℎ𝑖𝑐𝑙𝑒  =  𝐸𝑙𝑖𝑔𝑖𝑏𝑙𝑒𝐹𝑜𝑟𝑅𝑒𝑛𝑡𝑎𝑙 ∧  𝐼𝑑𝑙𝑒𝐷𝑎𝑦𝑠  ≥  14
The  fourteen-day  threshold  is  adopted  by  the  study  as  an  operational  
monitoring
 
rule
 
representing
 
two
 
consecutive
 
weekly
 
periods.
 
It
 
is
 
intended
 
to
 
distinguish
 
short
 
temporary
 
inactivity
 
from
 
more
 
meaningful
 
underutilization.
 
Idle
 
classification
 
shall
 
serve
 
only
 
as
 
an
 
administrative
 
indicator.
 
It
 
shall
 
not
 
automatically
 
cause
 
vehicle
 
transfer,
 
but
 
idle
 
vehicles
 
may
 
be
 
prioritized
 
for
 
review
 
when
 
another
 
branch
 
has
 
a
 
forecasted
 
shortage.
 
F.  Branch  Demand  Analysis  
Multi-location  fleet  operations  may  experience  uneven  demand  and  vehicle

when
 
one
 
location
 
receives
 
greater

while
 
another
 
retains

To
 
provide

proposed
 
system
 
may
 
calculate
 
the
 
proportion
 
of
 
qualifying
 
booking
 
activity
 
associated
 
with
 
each
 
branch.
 
Branch  share  shall  be  computed  as:  
 
𝐵𝑟𝑎𝑛𝑐ℎ𝑆ℎ𝑎𝑟𝑒
(

)
 
=
 
 𝐵𝑟𝑎𝑛𝑐ℎ𝐵𝑜𝑜𝑘𝑖𝑛𝑔𝑠

 ×  100

282   
This  metric  provides  a  descriptive  comparison  of  booking  activity  between

Branch

G.  Maintenance  Readiness  Determination  
Maintenance  status,  mileage,  vehicle  condition,  preventive  maintenance  
schedules,
 
and
 
historical
 
service
 
information
 
are
 
important
 
fleet-management
 
factors
 
because
 
a
 
vehicle
 
may
 
appear
 
available
 
in
 
the
 
booking
 
schedule
 
while
 
still
 
being
 
unsuitable
 
for
 
rental
 
operation.
 
Studies
 
on
 
vehicle
 
maintenance
 
and
 
fleet
 
management
 
support
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
mileage,
 
maintenance
 
records,
 
service
 
schedules,
 
and
 
vehicle
 
condition
 
as
 
factors
 
in
 
determining
 
fleet
 
readiness
 
and
 
preventing
 
operational
 
use
 
of
 
vehicles
 
requiring
 
maintenance
 
(Arena
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Chen
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Crespo
 
del
 
Castillo
 
&
 
Parlikad,
 
2024;
 
Chen
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025).
 
In  the  proposed  system,  maintenance  information  shall  be  treated  as  an  
operational
 
eligibility
 
condition
 
rather
 
than
 
as
 
an
 
arbitrary
 
numerical
 
score.
 
Before
 
a
 
vehicle
 
is
 
included
 
in
 
a
 
customer
 
recommendation,
 
vehicle
 
assignment,
 
or
 
branch-allocation
 
recommendation,
 
the
 
system
 
shall
 
determine
 
whether
 
the
 
vehicle
 
is
 
maintenance-ready.
 
A  vehicle  shall  be  treated  as  not  maintenance-ready  when  at  least  one  of  the  
following
 
conditions
 
exists:
 
an
 
active
 
maintenance
 
activity
 
remains
 
in
 
progress;
 
required
 
preventive
 
maintenance
 
is
 
due
 
or
 
overdue;
 
the
 
recorded
 
vehicle
 
condition
 
 

===== PAGE 290 =====
283   
indicates  that  the  vehicle  is  unsafe  or  unsuitable  for  rental;  or  an  unresolved  
maintenance
 
or
 
repair
 
concern
 
prevents
 
operational
 
use.
 
When  preventive  maintenance  is  monitored  using  date  and  mileage  criteria,  a  
vehicle
 
may
 
be
 
considered
 
due
 
for
 
maintenance
 
when:
 
 𝐶𝑢𝑟𝑟𝑒𝑛𝑡𝑂𝑑𝑜𝑚𝑒𝑡𝑒𝑟  ≥  𝑁𝑒𝑥𝑡𝑆𝑒𝑟𝑣𝑖𝑐𝑒𝑂𝑑𝑜𝑚𝑒𝑡𝑒𝑟
or  
 𝐶𝑢𝑟𝑟𝑒𝑛𝑡𝐷𝑎𝑡𝑒  ≥  𝑁𝑒𝑥𝑡𝑆𝑒𝑟𝑣𝑖𝑐𝑒𝐷𝑎𝑡𝑒
depending  on  the  applicable  maintenance  criteria  recorded  for  the  vehicle.  
A  vehicle  classified  as  not  maintenance-ready  shall  be  excluded  from  
Customer-Side
 
Vehicle
 
Recommendation
 
results
 
and
 
from
 
candidate
 
vehicles
 
considered
 
for
 
branch
 
transfer
 
until
 
the
 
relevant
 
maintenance
 
concern
 
has
 
been
 
completed
 
or
 
resolved.
 
H.  Travel  Distance,  Reference  Fuel  Efficiency,  and  Estimated  Fuel  Consumption  
Fuel  efficiency  and  estimated  fuel  consumption  may  serve  as  supporting  factors  
in
 
transportation
 
and
 
vehicle-selection
 
decisions
 
because
 
distance
 
and
 
vehicle
 
fuel
 
characteristics
 
influence
 
projected
 
trip
 
fuel
 
requirements
 
and
 
cost-related
 
considerations
 
(Vyas
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Moawad
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Şimşek
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025;
 
Alrashdi
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025).
 
In  this  study,  fuel  efficiency  shall  be  treated  as  estimated  or  reference  vehicle  
information
 
and
 
shall
 
be
 
expressed
 
in
 
kilometers
 
per
 
liter
 
(km/L).
 
The
 
reference
 
value
 
 

===== PAGE 291 =====
284   
may  be  based  on  manufacturer  specifications  or  owner-provided  vehicle  information.  
The
 
system
 
does
 
not
 
claim
 
to
 
continuously
 
measure
 
actual
 
real-world
 
fuel
 
efficiency.
 
When  travel-distance  information  is  available,  estimated  fuel  consumption  shall  
be
 
calculated
 
as:
 
 
𝐸𝑠𝑡𝑖𝑚𝑎𝑡𝑒𝑑𝐹𝑢𝑒𝑙𝐶𝑜𝑛𝑠𝑢𝑚𝑝𝑡𝑖𝑜𝑛
 
=
 
 𝑇𝑟𝑎𝑣𝑒𝑙𝐷𝑖𝑠𝑡𝑎𝑛𝑐𝑒
𝑘𝑚
𝑅𝑒𝑓𝑒𝑟𝑒𝑛𝑐𝑒𝐹𝑢𝑒𝑙𝐸𝑓𝑓𝑖𝑐𝑖𝑒𝑛𝑐𝑦
𝑘𝑚
/
𝐿
For  example,  if  an  estimated  trip  distance  is  250  kilometers  and  the  vehicle's  
recorded
 
reference
 
fuel
 
efficiency
 
is
 
10
 
km/L,
 
the
 
estimated
 
fuel
 
consumption
 
is:
 
 250  ÷  10  =  25  𝑙𝑖𝑡𝑒𝑟𝑠
The  calculated  value  represents  only  an  operational  estimate.  Actual  fuel  
consumption
 
may
 
vary
 
because
 
of
 
traffic,
 
driver
 
behavior,
 
passenger
 
or
 
cargo
 
load,
 
terrain,
 
weather,
 
road
 
condition,
 
vehicle
 
condition,
 
and
 
other
 
factors.
 
Fuel-related
 
information
 
shall
 
therefore
 
serve
 
only
 
as
 
supporting
 
decision
 
information
 
and
 
shall
 
not
 
independently
 
determine
 
final
 
vehicle
 
assignment
 
or
 
branch
 
allocation.
 
I.  Customer-Side  Vehicle  Recommendation  Logic  
Recommendation  systems  can  support  user  decision-making  by  matching  
available
 
options
 
with
 
user
 
preferences
 
or
 
requirements,
 
while
 
rule-based
 
approaches
 
are
 
appropriate
 
when
 
the
 
recommendation
 
criteria
 
must
 
remain
 
simple,
 
transparent,
 
and
 
interpretable
 
(Sabet
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Boteju
 
&
 
Munasinghe,
 
2020;
 
Prabowol
 
et
 
al.,
 
2019;
 
Haber
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026).
 
 

===== PAGE 292 =====
285   
In  the  proposed  system,  the  Customer-Side  Vehicle  Recommendation  shall  use  
rule-based
 
eligibility
 
filtering
 
followed
 
by
 
deterministic
 
ranking.
 
It
 
shall
 
not
 
use
 
an
 
unsupported
 
numerical
 
match
 
percentage
 
or
 
arbitrary
 
0–100
 
recommendation
 
score.
 
The  recommendation  process  shall  consist  of  two  stages:  eligibility  filtering  and  
ranking.
 
During  eligibility  filtering,  the  system  shall  exclude  vehicles  that  are  unavailable  
for
 
the
 
requested
 
rental
 
period,
 
inactive,
 
not
 
maintenance-ready,
 
or
 
affected
 
by
 
a
 
conflicting
 
booking
 
or
 
rental
 
schedule.
 
Passenger  capacity  shall  operate  as  a  hard  requirement:  
 𝑉𝑒ℎ𝑖𝑐𝑙𝑒𝐶𝑎𝑝𝑎𝑐𝑖𝑡𝑦 ≥ 𝑅𝑒𝑞𝑢𝑖𝑟𝑒𝑑𝑃𝑎𝑠𝑠𝑒𝑛𝑔𝑒𝑟𝐶𝑜𝑢𝑛𝑡
Vehicles  with  passenger  capacity  below  the  customer's  requirement  shall  be  
excluded.
 
     The  customer's  budget  shall  be  interpreted  as  the  total  base  rental  budget  for  the  
requested
 
duration.
 
Estimated
 
base
 
rental
 
cost
 
shall
 
be
 
computed
 
as:
 
 𝐸𝑠𝑡𝑖𝑚𝑎𝑡𝑒𝑑𝑅𝑒𝑛𝑡𝑎𝑙𝐶𝑜𝑠𝑡  =  𝐷𝑎𝑖𝑙𝑦𝑅𝑒𝑛𝑡𝑎𝑙𝑅𝑎𝑡𝑒  ×  𝑅𝑒𝑛𝑡𝑎𝑙𝐷𝑢𝑟𝑎𝑡𝑖𝑜𝑛
 A  vehicle  whose  estimated  base  rental  cost  exceeds  the  customer's  stated  
budget
 
shall
 
be
 
excluded.
 
Charges
 
that
 
cannot
 
yet
 
be
 
determined
 
during
 
the
 
recommendation
 
stage,
 
such
 
as
 
penalties
 
or
 
other
 
conditional
 
charges,
 
shall
 
not
 
be
 
included
 
in
 
the
 
base
 
estimate.
 
     After  eligibility  filtering,  the  remaining  vehicles  shall  be  ranked  using  predefined  
preference
 
rules.
 
A
 
vehicle
 
matching
 
the
 
customer's
 
preferred
 
vehicle
 
category
 
shall
 
be
 
 

===== PAGE 293 =====
286   
ranked  ahead  of  an  eligible  non-matching  category.  Where  several  vehicles  satisfy  the  
same
 
preference,
 
the
 
vehicle
 
with
 
the
 
closest
 
adequate
 
passenger
 
capacity
 
shall
 
be
 
preferred.
 
Remaining
 
ties
 
may
 
be
 
resolved
 
using
 
lower
 
estimated
 
base
 
rental
 
cost
 
and,
 
when
 
reliable
 
travel-distance
 
information
 
is
 
available,
 
better
 
reference
 
fuel
 
efficiency
 
or
 
lower
 
estimated
 
fuel
 
consumption.
 
     Destination  or  travel-area  information  may  be  used  to  obtain  applicable  
travel-distance
 
information
 
and
 
supporting
 
fuel
 
estimates
 
where
 
a
 
reliable
 
source
 
is
 
available.
 
If
 
the
 
corresponding
 
contextual
 
information
 
is
 
unavailable,
 
the
 
core
 
recommendation
 
process
 
shall
 
continue
 
using
 
the
 
remaining
 
customer
 
and
 
vehicle
 
criteria
 
and
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
the
 
unavailable
 
contextual
 
factor
 
was
 
not
 
evaluated.
 
     Instead  of  an  unsupported  match  score,  the  system  shall  provide  reasons  for  
recommending
 
a
 
vehicle,
 
such
 
as
 
matching
 
the
 
preferred
 
category,
 
satisfying
 
the
 
passenger
 
requirement,
 
remaining
 
within
 
the
 
stated
 
budget,
 
being
 
available
 
for
 
the
 
selected
 
dates,
 
and
 
being
 
maintenance-ready.
 
     Customer-Side  Vehicle  Recommendation  results  shall  serve  only  as  
vehicle-selection
 
guidance
 
before
 
booking
 
submission.
 
They
 
shall
 
not
 
constitute
 
booking
 
approval
 
or
 
guarantee
 
final
 
vehicle
 
assignment.

K.  Rule-Based  Branch  Allocation  Recommendation  
Rule-based  decision-support  systems  translate  operational  knowledge  and  
constraints
 
into
 
explicit
 
decision
 
conditions,
 
allowing
 
recommendations
 
to
 
remain
 
traceable
 
and
 
understandable
 
to
 
the
 
decision-maker
 
(Haber
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026;
 
Mavrin
 
&
 
Makarova,
 
2026;
 
Meira
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Mohamad
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026).
 
This
 
approach
 
is
 
appropriate
 
for
 
the
 
proposed
 
system
 
because
 
branch-allocation
 
outputs
 
are
 
intended
 
to
 
assist
 
rather
 
than
 
replace
 
administrative
 
judgment.
 
The  Branch  Allocation  Recommendation  shall  therefore  use  explicit  operational  
rules
 
instead
 
of
 
an
 
arbitrary
 
urgency
 
or
 
confidence
 
score.
 
Its
 
purpose
 
is
 
to
 
determine
 
whether
 
an
 
expected
 
shortage
 
at
 
one
 
branch
 
may
 
be
 
supported
 
by
 
an
 
available
 
surplus
 
at
 
another
 
branch.
 
A  potential  branch-transfer  recommendation  may  be  produced  when  the  
destination
 
branch
 
has
 
a
 
forecasted
 
shortage
 
for
 
a
 
vehicle
 
category
 
and
 
another
 
branch
 
has
 
a
 
forecasted
 
surplus
 
for
 
the
 
corresponding
 
category.
 
The  maximum  recommended  number  of  transfer  units  shall  be:  
 𝑅𝑒𝑐𝑜𝑚𝑚𝑒𝑛𝑑𝑒𝑑𝑇𝑟𝑎𝑛𝑠𝑓𝑒𝑟𝑈𝑛𝑖𝑡𝑠
𝑏
𝑠
,
 
𝑏
𝑑
,
 
𝑐
,
 
ℎ
 
 
 =  𝑚𝑖𝑛 ( 𝑆ℎ𝑜𝑟𝑡𝑎𝑔𝑒
𝑏
𝑑
,
 
𝑐
,
 
ℎ
,  𝑆𝑢𝑟𝑝𝑙𝑢𝑠
𝑏
𝑠
,
 
𝑐
,
 
ℎ
)
 

===== PAGE 296 =====
289   
For  example,  if  one  branch  has  a  forecasted  shortage  of  two  SUVs  while  
another
 
branch
 
has
 
a
 
surplus
 
of
 
three
 
SUVs,
 
the
 
system
 
may
 
recommend
 
transferring
 
up
 
to
 
two
 
eligible
 
SUVs.
 
Candidate  vehicles  from  the  source  branch  shall  be  active,  operationally  
available,
 
maintenance-ready,
 
and
 
free
 
of
 
conflicting
 
booking
 
or
 
rental
 
commitments
 
for
 
the
 
period
 
in
 
which
 
transfer
 
is
 
being
 
considered.
 
Among
 
multiple
 
eligible
 
vehicles,
 
those
 
with
 
the
 
longest
 
recorded
 
idle
 
duration
 
may
 
be
 
prioritized
 
for
 
administrative
 
review.
 
The  resulting  recommendation  shall  identify  the  source  branch,  destination  
branch,
 
vehicle
 
category,
 
forecast
 
requirement,
 
projected
 
supply,
 
shortage,
 
surplus,
 
recommended
 
transfer
 
quantity,
 
applicable
 
candidate
 
vehicle
 
or
 
vehicles,
 
and
 
the
 
operational
 
reasons
 
supporting
 
the
 
recommendation.
 
The  system  shall  not  automatically  change  a  vehicle's  branch  assignment.  The  
Owner/Admin
 
shall
 
review
 
the
 
generated
 
recommendation
 
and
 
decide
 
whether
 
it
 
should
 
be
 
approved,
 
rejected,
 
or
 
modified.
 
L.  Context-Aware  Operational  Assessment  
Context-aware  transportation  research  shows  that  road  condition,  route  
feasibility,
 
weather,
 
travel
 
distance,
 
and
 
related
 
environmental
 
or
 
operational
 
information
 
can
 
provide
 
useful
 
supporting
 
information
 
for
 
transport
 
decisions
 
(Liu
 
et
 
al.,
 
2022;
 
Dubey
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025;
 
Gabbar
 
et
 
al.,
 
2023).
 
Weather-aware
 
transportation
 
studies
 
also
 
demonstrate
 
that
 
precipitation,
 
visibility,
 
and
 
adverse
 
weather
 
conditions
 
may
 
affect
 
route
 
suitability
 
and
 
operational
 
decision-making
 
(Tobin
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Hussein
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021;
 
Čelar
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Pradeepraja
 
et
 
al.,
 
2025).
 
These
 
contextual
 
variables
 
can
 
be
 
integrated
 
through
 
external
 
APIs
 
or
 
other
 
supplementary
 
data
 
sources
 
where
 
 

===== PAGE 297 =====
290   
reliable  and  feasible  data  are  available  (Penumala  &  Naganaboina,  2025;  Sandhip  et  
al.,
 
2025;
 
Joshna
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026;
 
Vdovic
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021).
 
In  the  proposed  system,  contextual  information  shall  serve  as  supporting  
information
 
after
 
the
 
system
 
has
 
evaluated
 
its
 
primary
 
internal
 
operational
 
data,
 
including
 
forecasted
 
demand,
 
projected
 
supply,
 
vehicle
 
availability,
 
and
 
maintenance
 
readiness.
 
Applicable  contextual  information  may  include  weather  condition,  road  
condition,
 
route
 
feasibility,
 
route
 
accessibility,
 
travel
 
distance,
 
reference
 
fuel
 
efficiency,
 
and
 
estimated
 
fuel
 
consumption.
 
External  API  Selection  and  Fallback  Strategy  
The  proposed  system  will  use  selected  external  Application  Programming  
Interfaces
 
(APIs)
 
to
 
obtain
 
applicable
 
weather,
 
location,
 
routing,
 
and
 
incident-based
 
road
 
information
 
for
 
administrative
 
context-aware
 
decision
 
support.
 
The
 
API
 
providers
 
were
 
selected
 
based
 
on
 
the
 
contextual
 
information
 
required
 
by
 
the
 
proposed
 
system,
 
documented
 
service
 
capabilities,
 
geographic
 
coverage
 
applicable
 
to
 
the
 
Philippines,
 
implementation
 
feasibility,
 
and
 
the
 
availability
 
of
 
a
 
comparable
 
secondary
 
provider
 
that
 
can
 
serve
 
as
 
a
 
fallback.
 
External
 
API
 
information
 
will
 
remain
 
supporting
 
information
 
and
 
will
 
not
 
independently
 
determine
 
vehicle
 
assignment,
 
branch
 
allocation,
 
or
 
other
 
final
 
operational
 
actions.
 
To  reduce  dependence  on  a  single  external  service,  the  proposed  system  
adopts
 
a
 
primary-and-fallback
 
provider
 
strategy.
 
A
 
designated
 
primary
 
provider
 
will
 
normally
 
be
 
used
 
for
 
each
 
external
 
contextual
 
category.
 
The
 
corresponding
 
fallback
 
provider
 
will
 
only
 
be
 
requested
 
when
 
the
 
primary
 
provider
 
is
 
unavailable,
 
times
 
out,
 
 

===== PAGE 298 =====
291   
exceeds  an  applicable  service  limitation,  lacks  applicable  coverage,  or  returns  
unusable
 
or
 
insufficiently
 
current
 
information.
 
A
 
valid
 
adverse
 
result
 
returned
 
by
 
the
 
primary
 
provider,
 
such
 
as
 
a
 
road
 
closure
 
or
 
adverse
 
weather
 
condition,
 
shall
 
not
 
be
 
treated
 
as
 
provider
 
failure
 
and
 
shall
 
not
 
cause
 
the
 
system
 
to
 
request
 
the
 
fallback
 
provider
 
merely
 
to
 
obtain
 
a
 
different
 
result.

provide

Context  Classification  and  Normalization  Rules  
External  API  providers  return  raw  weather,  routing,  restriction,  and  incident  
information
 
using
 
provider-specific
 
formats.
 
To
 
ensure
 
that
 
contextual
 
information
 
is
 
interpreted
 
consistently
 
within
 
the
 
proposed
 
system,
 
applicable
 
external
 
and
 
authorized
 
manual
 
information
 
shall
 
be
 
normalized
 
into
 
predefined
 
operational
 
classifications
 
before
 
being
 
presented
 
as
 
administrative
 
decision-support
 
information.
 
These
 
classifications
 
are
 
adopted
 
by
 
the
 
study
 
for
 
system
 
implementation
 
and
 
do
 
not
 
represent
 
classifications
 
directly
 
produced
 
by
 
the
 
external
 
providers.
 
Weather  Classification.  Available  weather  information  shall  be  normalized  as  Normal,  
Caution,
 
Severe,
 
or
 
Unavailable
.
 
Normal
 
refers
 
to
 
generally
 
clear,
 
fair,
 
cloudy,
 
overcast,
 
mist,
 
or
 
other
 
available
 
conditions
 
without
 
a
 
significant
 
adverse-weather
 
indication
 
affecting
 
normal
 
travel.
 
Caution
 
refers
 
to
 
rain,
 
showers,
 
drizzle,
 
fog,
 
thunderstorms
 
without
 
an
 
identified
 
severe
 
condition,
 
or
 
another
 
reported
 
condition
 
that
 
may
 
affect
 
visibility,
 
traction,
 
or
 
normal
 
road
 
travel
 
but
 
does
 
not
 
independently
 
indicate
 
that
 
movement
 
should
 
not
 
proceed.
 
Severe
 
refers
 
to
 
severe
 
thunderstorms,
 
very
 
heavy
 
 

===== PAGE 303 =====
296   
precipitation,  tropical-cyclone-related  severe  weather,  an  applicable  official  
severe-weather
 
warning
 
or
 
alert,
 
or
 
another
 
reported
 
condition
 
that
 
may
 
materially
 
affect
 
safe
 
travel.
 
Unavailable
 
shall
 
be
 
used
 
when
 
sufficiently
 
current
 
and
 
usable
 
weather
 
information
 
cannot
 
be
 
obtained
 
from
 
either
 
the
 
primary
 
or
 
fallback
 
provider
 
and
 
no
 
reliable
 
authorized
 
manual
 
information
 
is
 
available.
 
Road-Condition  Classification.  For  purposes  of  the  proposed  system,  road  condition  
refers
 
to
 
the
 
incident-based
 
operational
 
state
 
of
 
an
 
applicable
 
route
 
rather
 
than
 
continuous
 
measurement
 
of
 
pavement
 
roughness,
 
potholes,
 
or
 
physical
 
road-surface
 
quality.
 
Road
 
condition
 
shall
 
be
 
represented
 
as
 
Open,
 
Caution,
 
Closed/Impassable,
 
or
 
Unknown
.
 
Open
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
an
 
applicable
 
route
 
is
 
available
 
and
 
no
 
relevant
 
blocking
 
or
 
cautionary
 
road
 
incident
 
has
 
been
 
identified.
 
Caution
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
the
 
route
 
remains
 
traversable
 
but
 
applicable
 
information
 
identifies
 
road
 
works,
 
lane
 
closures,
 
non-blocking
 
flooding,
 
accidents
 
affecting
 
movement,
 
dangerous
 
conditions,
 
or
 
another
 
relevant
 
non-blocking
 
disruption.
 
Closed/Impassable
 
shall
 
indicate
 
an
 
explicit
 
road
 
closure,
 
impassable
 
flooding,
 
blocking
 
restriction,
 
or
 
another
 
condition
 
preventing
 
the
 
applicable
 
road
 
movement.
 
Unknown
 
shall
 
be
 
used
 
when
 
sufficiently
 
reliable
 
incident
 
or
 
road-condition
 
information
 
cannot
 
be
 
obtained.
 
Route-Feasibility  Classification.  Route  feasibility  shall  be  represented  as  Feasible,  
Feasible
 
with
 
Caution,
 
Not
 
Feasible,
 
or
 
Unavailable
.
 
Feasible
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
a
 
valid
 
applicable
 
driving
 
route
 
is
 
available
 
and
 
no
 
blocking
 
or
 
significant
 
cautionary
 
condition
 
has
 
been
 
identified.
 
Feasible
 
with
 
Caution
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
a
 
valid
 
route
 
remains
 
available
 
but
 
applicable
 
weather,
 
road,
 
restriction,
 
or
 
incident
 
information
 
requires
 
administrative
 
attention.
 
Not
 
Feasible
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
available
 
information
 
confirms
 
that
 
the
 
proposed
 
movement
 
cannot
 
presently
 
proceed
 
because
 
of
 
an
 
 

===== PAGE 304 =====
297   
applicable  closure,  restriction,  or  impassable  route.  Unavailable  shall  indicate  that  
sufficient
 
routing
 
or
 
contextual
 
information
 
cannot
 
be
 
obtained
 
to
 
determine
 
feasibility.
 
Route-Accessibility  Classification.  Route  accessibility  shall  be  represented  as  
Accessible,
 
Limited,
 
Closed/Restricted,
 
or
 
Unknown
.
 
Accessible
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
an
 
applicable
 
route
 
is
 
available
 
without
 
a
 
material
 
restriction
 
affecting
 
normal
 
vehicle
 
movement.
 
Limited
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
the
 
route
 
remains
 
available
 
but
 
one
 
or
 
more
 
applicable
 
restrictions
 
or
 
incidents
 
may
 
limit
 
normal
 
movement.
 
Closed/Restricted
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
the
 
applicable
 
route
 
or
 
relevant
 
road
 
segment
 
is
 
closed,
 
blocked,
 
or
 
otherwise
 
restricted
 
against
 
the
 
proposed
 
movement.
 
Unknown
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
sufficient
 
accessibility
 
information
 
cannot
 
be
 
obtained.
 
Context  classifications  shall  supplement  rather  than  replace  the  system's  
internal
 
operational
 
analysis.
 
When
 
weather
 
is
 
Normal,
 
road
 
condition
 
is
 
Open,
 
route
 
feasibility
 
is
 
Feasible,
 
and
 
route
 
accessibility
 
is
 
Accessible,
 
an
 
applicable
 
allocation
 
recommendation
 
may
 
be
 
presented
 
without
 
an
 
additional
 
contextual
 
warning.
 
When
 
one
 
or
 
more
 
applicable
 
conditions
 
are
 
classified
 
as
 
Caution,
 
Severe,
 
Limited,
 
or
 
Feasible
 
with
 
Caution,
 
the
 
underlying
 
operational
 
recommendation
 
may
 
remain
 
available
 
but
 
shall
 
include
 
an
 
administrative
 
warning
 
requiring
 
review.
 
 
When  the  route  is  classified  as  Not  Feasible,  or  when  the  applicable  road  or  
accessibility
 
information
 
indicates
 
a
 
condition
 
that
 
prevents
 
movement,
 
the
 
system
 
shall
 
retain
 
the
 
underlying
 
demand,
 
supply,
 
shortage,
 
surplus,
 
and
 
allocation-recommendation
 
information
 
but
 
shall
 
indicate
 
that
 
the
 
proposed
 
movement
 
is
 
**not
 
presently
 
feasible**
 
under
 
the
 
recorded
 
conditions.
 
The
 
existence
 
of
 
a
 
 

===== PAGE 305 =====
298   
forecasted  shortage  shall  therefore  remain  distinguishable  from  the  current  feasibility  of  
performing
 
the
 
recommended
 
vehicle
 
transfer.

M.  Human  Decision  Control  and  Recommendation  Transparency  
Interpretability  and  traceability  are  important  advantages  of  rule-based  decision-support  
approaches
 
because
 
recommendations
 
can
 
be
 
linked
 
directly
 
to
 
the
 
conditions
 
that
 
produced
 
them
 
(Haber
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026;
 
Mohamad
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026).
 
Accordingly,
 
forecasting,
 
vehicle
 
utilization,
 
idle-vehicle
 
detection,
 
maintenance
 
readiness,
 
Customer-Side
 
 

===== PAGE 308 =====
301   
Vehicle  Recommendation,  and  Branch  Allocation  Recommendation  outputs  shall  be  
treated
 
as
 
decision-support
 
information.
 
The  proposed  system  shall  not  use  unsupported  confidence  classifications,  arbitrary  
urgency
 
values,
 
or
 
unexplained
 
0–100
 
recommendation
 
scores.
 
Instead,
 
recommendation
 
results
 
shall
 
present
 
traceable
 
reasons
 
based
 
on
 
the
 
rules
 
and
 
data
 
that
 
produced
 
the
 
recommendation.
 
For  example,  a  Branch  Allocation  Recommendation  may  indicate  that  the  destination  
branch
 
has
 
a
 
forecasted
 
shortage
 
of
 
two
 
vehicles,
 
the
 
source
 
branch
 
has
 
a
 
corresponding
 
surplus
 
of
 
three
 
vehicles,
 
the
 
proposed
 
vehicle
 
is
 
maintenance-ready,
 
has
 
no
 
conflicting
 
booking,
 
and
 
has
 
been
 
idle
 
for
 
the
 
defined
 
monitoring
 
period.
 
This  reason-oriented  approach  is  intended  to  make  system-generated  
recommendations
 
transparent
 
and
 
interpretable
 
while
 
preserving
 
final
 
operational
 
authority
 
under
 
the
 
Owner/Admin.
 
 
Development  Methodology  
This  study  will  use  a  developmental  and  descriptive  research  design  in  the  
development
 
and
 
evaluation
 
of
 
the
 
proposed
 
web-based
 
car
 
rental
 
management
 
system
 
with
 
demand
 
forecasting
 
and
 
context-aware
 
fleet
 
allocation.
 
The
 
study
 
is
 
developmental
 
because
 
it
 
focuses
 
on
 
designing,
 
developing,
 
testing,
 
and
 
evaluating
 
a
 
functional
 
system
 
that
 
addresses
 
the
 
identified
 
operational
 
problems
 
of
 
Briah’s
 
Car
 
Rental.
 
It
 
is
 
also
 
descriptive
 
because
 
it
 
examines
 
the
 
existing
 
business
 
workflow
 
and
 
evaluates
 
the
 
developed
 
system
 
based
 
on
 
selected
 
quality
 
and
 
acceptability
 
criteria.
 
 

===== PAGE 309 =====
302   
For  the  software  development  process,  the  study  will  use  the  Iterative  Waterfall  
Model.
 
This
 
model
 
was
 
selected
 
because
 
the
 
proposed
 
system
 
requires
 
clear
 
documentation,
 
organized
 
development
 
phases,
 
structured
 
system
 
design
 
outputs,
 
and
 
systematic
 
testing
 
before
 
evaluation.
 
At
 
the
 
same
 
time,
 
its
 
iterative
 
nature
 
allows
 
the
 
researchers
 
to
 
revisit
 
earlier
 
phases
 
when
 
revisions
 
are
 
needed
 
based
 
on
 
adviser
 
feedback,
 
client
 
validation,
 
prototype
 
review,
 
testing
 
results,
 
or
 
evaluation
 
findings.
 
The  use  of  a  structured  development  methodology  is  appropriate  for  this  study  
because
 
the
 
proposed
 
system
 
includes
 
several
 
connected
 
modules,
 
such
 
as
 
booking
 
management,
 
customer
 
and
 
requirement
 
verification,
 
payment
 
verification,
 
vehicle
 
and
 
fleet
 
management,
 
rental
 
transaction
 
management,
 
maintenance
 
monitoring,
 
report
 
generation,
 
demand
 
forecasting,
 
rule-based
 
recommendation,
 
context-aware
 
decision
 
support,
 
and
 
role-based
 
access
 
control.
 
Related
 
studies
 
show
 
that
 
manual
 
and
 
semi-manual
 
car
 
rental
 
operations
 
commonly
 
experience
 
delays,
 
fragmented
 
records,
 
overlapping
 
reservations,
 
and
 
poor
 
vehicle
 
availability
 
monitoring
 
(Dhami
 
et
 
al.,
 
2024;
 
Mhatre
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026;
 
Rahman
 
&
 
Mohd
 
Rusli,
 
2023).
 
In
 
addition,
 
prior
 
studies
 
support
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
historical
 
operational
 
data
 
for
 
demand
 
forecasting
 
and
 
the
 
use
 
of
 
rule-based
 
decision-support
 
approaches
 
for
 
improving
 
vehicle
 
allocation
 
and
 
operational
 
planning
 
(Yu
 
et
 
al.,
 
2020;
 
Vateekul
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021;
 
Liu
 
et
 
al.,
 
2021;
 
You
 
&
 
Hsieh,
 
2025;
 
Labib
 
et
 
al.,
 
2026).
 
The  development  and  evaluation  of  the  system  will  also  consider  security,  
usability,
 
reliability,
 
and
 
overall
 
software
 
quality.
 
Since
 
the
 
system
 
will
 
handle
 
customer
 
records,
 
uploaded
 
requirements,
 
proof
 
of
 
payment,
 
booking
 
records,
 
fleet
 
information,
 
and
 
operational
 
reports,
 
secure
 
authentication
 
and
 
role-based
 
access
 
control
 
will
 
be
 
implemented
 
to
 
protect
 
sensitive
 
information
 
and
 
restrict
 
access
 
based
 
on
 
user
 
roles
 
 

===== PAGE 310 =====
303   
such  as  administrator,  owner,  and  staff  (Dedhia,  2026;  Lu  et  al.,  2024).  The  system  will  
also
 
include
 
role-based
 
access
 
restrictions
 
to
 
ensure
 
that
 
only
 
authorized
 
users
 
can
 
access
 
modules
 
related
 
to
 
booking
 
approval,
 
payment
 
verification,
 
reports
 
generation,
 
user
 
management,
 
and
 
operational
 
monitoring.
 
The  completed  system  will  undergo  unit  testing,  integration  testing,  security  and  
access
 
control
 
testing,
 
system
 
testing,
 
and
 
user
 
acceptance
 
testing
 
to
 
verify
 
whether
 
the
 
developed
 
modules
 
function
 
correctly
 
both
 
individually
 
and
 
as
 
an
 
integrated
 
system.
 
Unit
 
testing
 
will
 
separately
 
evaluate
 
the
 
customer
 
web
 
application,
 
admin
 
or
 
owner
 
web
 
application,
 
and
 
staff
 
web
 
application
 
to
 
ensure
 
that
 
each
 
module
 
performs
 
according
 
to
 
its
 
intended
 
functionality.
 
Integration
 
testing
 
will
 
verify
 
proper
 
interaction
 
between
 
connected
 
modules,
 
including
 
role-based
 
access
 
behavior
 
across
 
different
 
user
 
accounts
 
and
 
permissions.
 
Security
 
and
 
access
 
control
 
testing
 
will
 
assess

protected

and

restrictions
 
to
 
ensure
 
that

sensitive

and

is
 
prevented.
 
User

will

be
 
conducted
 
with
 
selected
 
users
 
to
 
determine
 
whether
 
the
 
developed
 
system
 
aligns
 
with
 
the
 
operational

and
 
requirements
 
of
 
the
 
business.
 
The  completed  system  will  be  evaluated  using  selected  ISO/IEC  25010  
software
 
quality
 
characteristics,
 
which
 
provide
 
a
 
structured
 
basis
 
for
 
assessing
 
web-based
 
and
 
multi-module
 
information
 
systems
 
(Monteverde
 
et
 
al.,
 
2023;
 
Bondoc,
 
2023;
 
Mendoza
 
et
 
al.,

Process  Model  
 

===== PAGE 311 =====
304   
The  proponents  will  use  the  Iterative  Waterfall  Model  as  the  process  model  for  
the
 
development
 
of
 
the
 
proposed
 
system.
 
The
 
model
 
follows
 
a
 
structured
 
sequence
 
of
 
phases,
 
namely
 
requirements
 
analysis,
 
system
 
design,
 
implementation,
 
testing
 
and
 
validation,
 
deployment
 
preparation,
 
and
 
maintenance
 
planning.
 
Unlike
 
the
 
strictly
 
linear
 
waterfall
 
approach,
 
the
 
iterative
 
waterfall
 
model
 
allows
 
the
 
researchers
 
to
 
return
 
to
 
earlier
 
phases
 
when
 
improvements
 
or
 
corrections
 
are
 
needed.
 
This
 
makes
 
the
 
model
 
suitable
 
for
 
the
 
study
 
because
 
the
 
system
 
may
 
require
 
refinement
 
based
 
on
 
adviser
 
feedback,
 
client
 
validation,
 
prototype
 
review,
 
testing
 
results,
 
and
 
evaluation
 
findings.
 
 
Figure  60.   Iterative  Waterfall  Model  
 
The  first  phase  is  requirements  analysis.  In  this  phase,  the  researchers  will  
gather
 
and
 
analyze
 
information
 
from
 
client
 
interviews,
 
workflow
 
validation,
 
observation,
 
and
 
document
 
review
 
to
 
identify
 
the
 
functional
 
and
 
non-functional
 
requirements
 
of
 
the
 
system.
 
The  second  phase  is  system  design.  In  this  phase,  the  researchers  will  prepare  
the
 
system
 
architecture,
 
use
 
case
 
diagrams,
 
activity
 
diagrams,
 
database
 
schema,
 
data
 
dictionary,
 
and
 
user
 
interface
 
designs.
 
 


===== PAGE 312 =====
305   
The  third  phase  is  implementation.  In  this  phase,  the  system  modules  will  be  
developed,
 
including
 
booking
 
management,
 
requirement
 
verification,
 
payment
 
verification,
 
vehicle
 
and
 
fleet
 
management,
 
rental
 
monitoring,
 
maintenance
 
monitoring,
 
reports
 
generation,
 
demand
 
forecasting,
 
rule-based
 
recommendation,
 
and
 
context-aware
 
decision
 
support.
 
The  fourth  phase  is  testing  and  validation.  In  this  phase,  the  developed  modules  
will
 
be
 
checked
 
through
 
unit
 
testing,
 
integration
 
testing,
 
and
 
system
 
testing
 
to
 
determine
 
whether
 
they
 
meet
 
the
 
identified
 
requirements.
 
The  fifth  phase  is  deployment  preparation.  In  this  phase,  the  system  will  be  
prepared
 
for
 
pilot
 
use
 
by
 
configuring
 
user
 
roles,
 
database
 
records,
 
access
 
permissions,
 
and
 
basic
 
user
 
guidance.
 
The  final  phase  is  maintenance  planning.  In  this  phase,  possible  improvements,  
error
 
corrections,
 
backup
 
procedures,
 
security
 
updates,
 
and
 
future
 
enhancements
 
will
 
be
 
identified.

===== PAGE 315 =====

Decision-Support  Development  
Forecasting  and  Recommendation  Logic  
TypeScript-Based  Weighted  Moving  Average  and  Rule-Based  Recommendation  Logic

Open-Meteo  Forecast  API;  OpenWeather  One  Call  API  3.0   
Open-Meteo  serves  as  the  primary  weather-data  provider,  while  OpenWeather  serves  as  the  fallback  provider  for  applicable  weather  information  used  by  the  administrative  context-aware  decision-support  component.  
Context-Aware  Decision  Support  
Location,  Routing,  and  Road-Incident  APIs  
TomTom  Orbis  APIs;  HERE  Technologies  APIs  
TomTom  Orbis  location,  routing,  and  traffic  services  serve  as  the  primary  provider  for  applicable  destination  processing,  route-distance  and  travel-time  estimation,  route  assessment,  and  incident-based  road  information,  while  corresponding  HERE  Technologies  services  serve  as  fallback  providers.

Component  Testing  Tool

Test  Case  Description  
Test  cases  will  be  prepared  to  guide  the  testing  of  the  system’s  major  modules  
and
 
workflows.
 
Each
 
test
 
case
 
will
 
include
 
the
 
module
 
or
 
feature
 
to
 
be
 
tested,
 
test
 
objective,
 
input
 
data,
 
expected
 
output,
 
actual
 
output,
 
result
 
status,
 
and
 
remarks.
 
The
 
test
 
cases
 
will
 
be
 
based
 
on
 
the
 
identified
 
system
 
requirements,
 
use
 
case
 
reports,
 
activity
 
diagrams,
 
and
 
system
 
workflows.
 
The  test  cases  will  cover  both  valid  and  invalid  inputs  to  determine  whether  the  
system
 
can
 
process
 
correct
 
data,
 
reject
 
incomplete
 
or
 
incorrect
 
entries,
 
display
 
appropriate
 
messages,
 
and
 
store
 
records
 
accurately.
 
The
 
test
 
cases
 
will
 
also
 
include
 
role-based
 
scenarios
 
to
 
verify
 
whether
 
customers,
 
staff,
 
administrators,
 
and
 
owners
 
can
 
only
 
access
 
the
 
features
 
and
 
records
 
assigned
 
to
 
their
 
roles.

The  Customer/Renter  Survey  Questionnaire  was  designed  to  assess  
user-facing
 
quality
 
characteristics
 
of
 
the
 
system
 
based
 
on
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010.
 
It
 
focuses
 
on

Capability,

which
 
are
 
critical
 
in

customers’
 
experience
 
in
 
using
 
the
 
booking
 
and
 
vehicle
 
selection
 
features
 
of
 
the
 
system.

A.  Functional  Suitability  
In  the  ISO/IEC  25010  definition,  functional  suitability  refers  to  how  well  a  
product
 
or
 
system
 
provides
 
the
 
right
 
functions
 
to
 
meet
 
user
 
needs
 
under
 
specified
 
conditions,
 
ensuring
 
that
 
all
 
required
 
functions
 
are
 
present,
 
work
 
correctly,
 
and
 
effectively
 
support
 
users
 
in
 
achieving
 
their
 
intended
 
tasks
 
and
 
objectives.
 
The  following  statements  were  used  as  part  of  the  survey  instrument:  
●  The  system  provides  all  the  features  needed  for  submitting  a  car  rental  booking  
request.
 
●  The  vehicle  catalog  allows  customers  to  view  available  vehicles  clearly.  
●  The  Customer-side  Vehicle  Recommendationr  allows  customers  to  input  trip  
requirements
 
before
 
booking.
 
●  The  Customer-side  Vehicle  Recommendation  provides  vehicle  suggestions  that  
match
 
the
 
customer’s
 
passenger
 
capacity,
 
destination
 
or
 
travel
 
area,
 
budget,
 
rental
 
duration,
 
and
 
vehicle
 
preference.
 
●  The  system  allows  customers  to  upload  rental  requirements  and  proof  of  
payment
 
properly.
 
●  The  system  allows  customers  to  track  their  booking  status.  
These  questions  specifically  address  whether  the  system  offers  complete  and  
appropriate
 
customer-side
 
features,
 
covers
 
essential
 
booking
 
functions,
 
and
 
supports
 
vehicle
 
selection
 
before
 
booking.
 
By
 
directly
 
targeting
 
these
 
functional
 
areas,
 
the
 
questions
 
together
 
measure
 
the
 
system’s
 
functional
 
suitability
 
from
 
the
 
customer
 
perspective.

●  The  system  applies  role-based  access  control.  
●  The  system  protects  sensitive  customer,  payment,  and  operational  records.  
●  The  system  records  important  user  actions  through  logs  or  audit  records.  
These  questions  are  included  to  determine  whether  the  system  meets  the  
expected
 
technical
 
quality
 
requirements
 
under
 
this
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
characteristic.
 
G.  Maintainability  
The  following  statements  were  used  as  part  of  the  IT  expert  evaluation  instrument:  
●  The  system  structure  supports  future  modification.  
●  The  system  modules  are  organized  and  manageable.  
●  The  system  can  be  corrected  or  updated  without  affecting  unrelated  functions.  
●  The  database  structure  supports  proper  record  management.  
These  questions  are  included  to  determine  whether  the  system  meets  the  
expected
 
technical
 
quality
 
requirements
 
under
 
this
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
characteristic.

===== PAGE 352 =====
345   
The  following  statements  were  used  as  part  of  the  IT  expert  evaluation  instrument:

●  The  system  can  support  future  enhancements.  
●  The  system  can  accommodate  changes  in  rules,  policies,  and  operational  data.  
●  The  system  can  be  adapted  to  changes  in  data  source  availability.

I.  Safety

●  The  system  prevents  unauthorized  users  from  accessing  restricted  functions.  
●  The  system  supports  human-controlled  approval  for  booking,  payment,  and  
vehicle
 
assignment.
 
●  The  system  provides  system  behavior  that  supports  safe  and  controlled  
operations.
 
These  questions  are  included  to  determine  whether  the  system  meets  the

Validity  and  Reliability  of  the  Instrument  
The  evaluation  instrument  will  undergo  validation  before  it  is  administered  to  the  
final
 
respondents.
 
Since
 
the
 
study
 
will
 
use
 
an
 
existing
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010-based
 
evaluation
 
instrument
 
as
 
a
 
guide,
 
the
 
researchers
 
will
 
adapt
 
the
 
items
 
according
 
to
 
the
 
selected
 
quality
 
characteristics,
 
system
 
features,
 
and
 
respondent
 
groups.
 
The
 
adapted
 
questionnaire
 
will
 
be
 
reviewed
 
by
 
qualified
 
validators
 
to
 
determine
 
whether
 
the
 
items
 
 

===== PAGE 353 =====
346   
are  clear,  relevant,  aligned  with  the  selected  ISO/IEC  25010  characteristics,  and  
appropriate
 
for
 
the
 
intended
 
respondents.
 
The  validators  may  include  an  IT  expert,  a  research  or  statistics  validator,  and  a  
language
 
or
 
grammar
 
validator.
 
The
 
IT
 
expert
 
may
 
review
 
the
 
technical
 
relevance
 
of
 
the
 
items,
 
the
 
research
 
or
 
statistics
 
validator
 
may
 
review
 
the
 
appropriateness
 
of
 
the
 
instrument
 
and
 
scale,
 
and
 
the
 
language
 
or
 
grammar
 
validator
 
may
 
review
 
the
 
clarity
 
and
 
wording
 
of
 
the
 
questionnaire.
 
Revisions
 
will
 
be
 
made
 
based
 
on
 
their
 
comments
 
and
 
suggestions
 
before
 
the
 
final
 
administration
 
of
 
the
 
instrument.
 
To  establish  reliability,  the  revised  questionnaire  may  be  pilot-tested  with  
respondents
 
who
 
are
 
not
 
part
 
of
 
the
 
final
 
evaluation
 
group.
 
The
 
responses
 
from
 
the
 
pilot
 
test
 
may
 
be
 
analyzed
 
using
 
Cronbach’s
 
Alpha
 
to
 
determine
 
the
 
internal
 
consistency
 
of
 
the
 
instrument.
 
If
 
the
 
reliability
 
result
 
does
 
not
 
meet
 
the
 
acceptable
 
threshold
 
required
 
by
 
the
 
adviser
 
or
 
panel,
 
the
 
questionnaire
 
items
 
will
 
be
 
reviewed,
 
revised,
 
or
 
removed
 
before
 
final
 
administration.
 
 
Ethical  Considerations  
Ethical  considerations  will  be  observed  throughout  the  conduct  of  the  study  to  
ensure
 
responsible,
 
respectful,
 
and
 
lawful
 
handling
 
of
 
data
 
during
 
requirements
 
gathering,
 
system
 
development,
 
testing,
 
and
 
evaluation.
 
Since
 
the
 
proposed
 
system
 
will
 
involve
 
customer
 
information,
 
booking
 
records,
 
uploaded
 
requirements,
 
proof
 
of
 
payment,
 
vehicle
 
records,
 
rental
 
transactions,
 
maintenance
 
records,
 
and
 
operational
 
reports,
 
the
 
proponents
 
will
 
ensure
 
that
 
data
 
privacy
 
and
 
confidentiality
 
are
 
maintained.
 
Before  conducting  interviews,  surveys,  testing,  or  evaluation,  the  participants  
will
 
be
 
informed
 
about
 
the
 
purpose
 
of
 
the
 
study
 
and
 
their
 
role
 
in
 
the
 
research.
 
Participation
 
will
 
be
 
voluntary,
 
and
 
respondents
 
will
 
have
 
the
 
right
 
to
 
refuse
 
or
 
withdraw
 
 

===== PAGE 354 =====
347   
from  the  activity  without  any  penalty.  The  information  gathered  from  respondents  will  be  
used
 
only
 
for
 
academic
 
and
 
system
 
development
 
purposes.
 
The  study  will  also  observe  the  principles  of  the  Data  Privacy  Act  of  2012,  or  
Republic
 
Act
 
No.
 
10173.
 
Personal
 
information
 
and
 
sensitive
 
records
 
will
 
not
 
be
 
publicly
 
disclosed
 
without
 
permission.
 
If
 
actual
 
client
 
data
 
or
 
customer
 
records
 
are
 
needed
 
for
 
testing,
 
the
 
proponents
 
will
 
minimize,
 
mask,
 
or
 
anonymize
 
identifiable
 
information
 
whenever
 
possible.
 
If
 
actual
 
historical
 
rental
 
data
 
is
 
incomplete
 
or
 
unavailable,
 
sample
 
or
 
simulated
 
data
 
may
 
be
 
used
 
for
 
testing
 
the
 
forecasting,
 
vehicle
 
utilization
 
analysis,
 
and
 
allocation
 
recommendation
 
features.
 
The  proposed  system  will  also  be  designed  to  protect  data  through  secure  
authentication,
 
role-based
 
access
 
control,
 
and
 
restricted
 
access
 
to
 
sensitive
 
records.
 
Customers
 
will
 
only
 
be
 
allowed
 
to
 
access
 
their
 
own
 
account,
 
booking
 
details,
 
uploaded
 
requirements,
 
and
 
payment
 
proof.
 
Administrators
 
will
 
be
 
allowed
 
to
 
manage
 
operational
 
records
 
based
 
on
 
their
 
assigned
 
responsibilities.
 
Owners
 
will
 
have
 
access
 
to
 
financial,
 
reporting,
 
and
 
decision-support
 
functions.
 
The  study  will  not  include  unauthorized  tracking  of  vehicles  or  individuals.  
Although
 
the
 
client
 
uses
 
existing
 
tools
 
such
 
as
 
AKSH
 
GPS,
 
Apple
 
Find
 
My,
 
and
 
AirTags,
 
the
 
proposed
 
system
 
will
 
not
 
directly
 
integrate
 
live
 
GPS
 
tracking.
 
The
 
system
 
will
 
only
 
record
 
vehicle
 
status,
 
monitoring
 
notes,
 
rental
 
status,
 
condition
 
records,
 
and
 
maintenance-related
 
updates
 
entered
 
by
 
authorized
 
users.
 
This
 
ensures
 
that
 
the
 
system
 
remains
 
aligned
 
with
 
its
 
declared
 
scope
 
and
 
avoids
 
privacy
 
concerns
 
related
 
to
 
live
 
location
 
monitoring.
 
The  proponents  will  also  maintain  academic  integrity  by  properly  citing  all  
related
 
literature,
 
studies,
 
systems,
 
frameworks,
 
and
 
standards
 
used
 
in
 
the
 
manuscript.

The  proponents  will  not  fabricate  data,  manipulate  evaluation  results,  or  claim  features

in

Data  Analysis  (Procedure  and  Treatment)  
After  the  system  evaluation,  the  collected  questionnaire  responses  will  be  
checked,
 
tabulated,
 
and
 
analyzed
 
according
 
to
 
respondent
 
group
 
and
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
quality
 
characteristics.
 
The
 
responses
 
of
 
customer
 
or
 
renter
 
respondents
 
will
 
be
 
used
 
to
 
evaluate
 
customer-side
 
features
 
such
 
as
 
account
 
registration,
 
Customer-side
 
Vehicle
 
Recommendation,
 
vehicle
 
browsing,
 
booking
 
request
 
submission,
 
requirement
 
upload,
 
proof-of-payment
 
submission,
 
booking
 
status
 
tracking,
 
notifications,
 
rental
 
policy
 
viewing,
 
and
 
ease
 
of
 
use.
 
The  responses  of  owner,  administrator,  and  staff  respondents  will  be  used  to  
evaluate
 
administrative
 
and
 
operational
 
features
 
such
 
as
 
booking
 
management,
 
customer
 
verification,
 
payment
 
validation,
 
vehicle
 
records,
 
maintenance
 
monitoring,
 
fleet
 
monitoring,
 
reports,
 
demand
 
forecasting,
 
branch
 
allocation
 
recommendation,
 
and
 
role-based
 
access
 
control.
 
The
 
responses
 
of
 
IT
 
expert
 
evaluators
 
will
 
be
 
used
 
to
 
assess
 
technical
 
quality
 
characteristics
 
such
 
as
 
functional
 
suitability,
 
performance
 
efficiency,
 
compatibility,
 
interaction
 
capability,
 
reliability,
 
security,
 
maintainability,
 
flexibility,
 
and
 
safety.
 
The  data  gathered  from  the  evaluation  questionnaire  will  be  analyzed  using  
frequency
 
count,
 
percentage,
 
weighted
 
mean,
 
ranking,
 
and
 
verbal
 
interpretation.
 
Frequency
 
count
 
and
 
percentage
 
will
 
be
 
used
 
to
 
summarize
 
respondent
 
profiles
 
and
 
response
 
distribution.
 
Weighted
 
means
 
will
 
be
 
used
 
to
 
determine
 
the
 
level
 
of
 
acceptability
 
of
 
the
 
system
 
based
 
on
 
each
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
quality
 
characteristic.
 
 

===== PAGE 356 =====
349   
Ranking  will  be  used  to  identify  the  highest  and  lowest-rated  quality  characteristics  or  
system
 
features.
 
If  actual  and  forecasted  demand  values  are  available,  forecasting  accuracy  may  
be
 
evaluated
 
using
 
Mean
 
Absolute
 
Percentage
 
Error.
 
However,
 
if
 
actual
 
post-implementation
 
demand
 
data
 
are
 
not

during
 
the

stage,

forecasting
 
output
 
will
 
be
 
treated
 
as
 
a
 
functional
 
demonstration
 
of
 
the
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
feature
 
rather
 
than
 
proof
 
of
 
long-term
 
operational
 
effectiveness.

350   
 
Frequency  Count  
Frequency  count  will  be  used  to  determine  the  number  of  respondents  who  
selected
 
each
 
rating
 
in
 
the
 
evaluation
 
questionnaire.
 
This
 
will
 
help
 
identify
 
how
 
many
 
respondents
 
rated
 
each
 
statement
 
as
 
strongly
 
agree,
 
agree,
 
disagree,
 
or
 
strongly
 
disagree.
 
Formula:  
f  =  number  of  responses  per  rating  
Where:  
f  =  frequency  or  number  of  responses  
 
Percentage  
Percentage  will  be  used  to  determine  the  proportion  of  responses  for  each  
rating
 
in
 
relation
 
to
 
the
 
total
 
number
 
of
 
respondents.
 
Formula:  
P  =  (f  /  N)  ×  100  
Where:  
P  =  percentage  
 
f
 
=
 
frequency
 
of
 
responses
 
 
N
 
=
 
total
 
number
 
of
 
respondents
 
 
100
 
=
 
constant
 
value
 
 
Weighted  Mean  
Weighted  mean  will  be  used  to  determine  the  average  rating  for  each  
evaluation
 
indicator
 
and
 
each
 
ISO/IEC
 
25010
 
quality
 
characteristic.
 
 

===== PAGE 358 =====
351   
Formula:  
WM  =  Σ fx  /  N  
Where:  
WM  =  weighted  mean  
 
Σ
 
=
 
summation
 
symbol
 
 
f
 
=
 
frequency
 
of
 
responses
 
 
x
 
=
 
assigned
 
numerical
 
value
 
or
 
weight
 
of
 
each
 
rating
 
 
N
 
=
 
total
 
number
 
of
 
respondents
 
The  computed  weighted  mean  will  be  interpreted  using  the  four-point  Likert  
scale
 
interpretation
 
table.
 
 
Ranking  
Ranking  will  be  used  to  identify  which  ISO/IEC  25010  quality  characteristics  
received
 
the
 
highest
 
and
 
lowest
 
ratings.
 
The
 
quality
 
characteristic
 
with
 
the
 
highest
 
weighted
 
mean
 
will
 
be
 
ranked
 
first,
 
while
 
the
 
characteristic
 
with
 
the
 
lowest
 
weighted
 
mean
 
will
 
be
 
ranked
 
last.
 
This
 
will
 
help
 
the
 
proponents
 
determine
 
the
 
strongest
 
and
 
weakest
 
areas
 
of
 
the
 
system
 
based
 
on
 
the
 
evaluation
 
results.
 
 
Mean  Absolute  Percentage  Error  
Mean  Absolute  Percentage  Error  (MAPE)  may  be  used  to  evaluate  the  
observed
 
error
 
of
 
the
 
Weighted
 
Moving
 
Average
 
demand
 
forecasts
 
when
 
corresponding
 
actual
 
qualifying
 
demand
 
becomes
 
available.
 
For
 
the
 
primary
 
forecasting
 
evaluation,
 
forecast
 
and
 
actual
 
values
 
shall
 
refer
 
to
 
the
 
same
 
branch,
 
vehicle
 
category,
 
and
 
weekly
 
period,
 
and
 
the
 
forecast
 
used
 
shall
 
be
 
the
 
value
 
originally
 
generated
 
when
 
the
 
target
 
period
 
was
 
one
 
week
 
ahead.
 
This
 
approach
 
maintains
 
a
 
 

===== PAGE 359 =====
352   
consistent  forecast  horizon  for  error  comparison.  MAPE  shall  include  only  observations  
where
 
actual
 
demand
 
is
 
greater
 
than
 
zero
 
because
 
percentage
 
error
 
is
 
undefined
 
when
 
the
 
actual
 
value
 
is
 
zero.
 
Periods
 
with
 
zero
 
actual
 
demand
 
shall
 
be
 
reported
 
separately
 
and
 
excluded
 
from
 
the
 
MAPE
 
divisor.
 
Lower
 
MAPE
 
values
 
indicate
 
smaller
 
observed
 
forecasting
 
errors,
 
and
 
no
 
qualitative
 
accuracy
 
classification
 
shall
 
be
 
assigned
 
unless
 
a
 
verified
 
interpretation
 
basis
 
is
 
formally
 
adopted.
 
Formula:  
MAPE  =  (1/n)  Σ  |(Actual  Value  -  Forecasted  Value)  /  Actual  Value|  ×  100  
Where:  
n  =  number  of  observations  
 
Actual
 
Value
 
=
 
actual
 
demand
 
value
 
 
Forecasted
 
Value
 
=
 
demand
 
value
 
estimated
 
by
 
the
 
system
